Result and discussion

The results clearly demonstrate that the process CNC Machining \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(K_2)$$\end{document} is an exceptionally suitable manufacturing method for all types of materials. The graphical representation of these results is presented in the accompanying Fig. 4.

Effectiveness test

The suggested LDMF entropy method has been tested on three test criteria to show its validity and reliability.

Test Criterion 1: An efficient decision-making process should not change the best alternative when a suboptimal alternative is substituted with a worse alternative (provided that the relative relevance of each decision criterion remains unchanged).Test Criterion 2: The transitivity principle should be adhered to when ranking options through the use of an effective decision-making procedure.Test Criterion 3: The innovative overall ordering of the alternatives should match the original overall ordering of the unresolved problem when the same decision-making procedure is applied to acquire the ordering of many sub-problems of the decision-making problem.Effectiveness for Test Criterion 1: If we interchange the values MS with NMS and RMS with RNMS, the ranking of the alternatives is still \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_2$$\end{document}. It shows the effectiveness under Test criterion 1.Effectiveness for Test Criterion 2,3: First, we take subproblems with alternatives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_2$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_3$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_2$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_3$$\end{document}. By utilizing same entropy method, we rank the 3 subproblems as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_2 > K_1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_1 > K_3$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_2 > K_3$$\end{document}, which are similar with overall ranking \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_2> K_1 > K_3$$\end{document}. Thus, it is effective under Test Criterion 2,3.

Application on inclusion measure

Algorithm

Step 1: Define an alternative and parameter set.Step 2: Construct LDMFS for each alternative(\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q_i$$\end{document}) and known pattern Q.Step 3: Compute the inclusion measures \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {I}$$\end{document}7 for each alternative with Q.Step 4: Rank the alternatives based on divergence measures.Step 5: A higher value indicates the greater possibility of inclusion.

Step 1: Define an alternative and parameter set.

Step 1: Define an alternative and parameter set.

Step 2: Construct LDMFS for each alternative(\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q_i$$\end{document}) and known pattern Q.

Step 2: Construct LDMFS for each alternative(\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q_i$$\end{document}) and known pattern Q.

Step 3: Compute the inclusion measures \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {I}$$\end{document}7 for each alternative with Q.

Step 3: Compute the inclusion measures \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {I}$$\end{document}7 for each alternative with Q.

Step 4: Rank the alternatives based on divergence measures.

Step 4: Rank the alternatives based on divergence measures.

Step 5: A higher value indicates the greater possibility of inclusion.

Step 5: A higher value indicates the greater possibility of inclusion.

Results and discussion

The results indicate that pattern \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q_5$$\end{document} matches the pattern of Q when compared to other unidentified patterns. The graphical representation of these results is shown in Fig. 5.

Fig. 5Graphical Representation of Outcomes with their Inclusion Measures.

Graphical Representation of Outcomes with their Inclusion Measures.

Superiority analysis

This section examines the excellence of our study from three different angles: theoretical, practical, and methodological. It also includes the constraints and managerial ramifications of our suggested theory.

Dominance of LDMFS

The superiority of LDMFS is demonstrated in Table 17, which compares the proposed LDMFS with several existing fuzzy concepts. The table reveals that previous fuzzy sets lack reference parameters. In contrast, our proposed LDMFS, with its multi-dimensional approach, simplifies the calculation process and facilitates the application of complex systems of problems within a single model.

Supremacy of information measures

Similarity measures: Flexible comparison: Permit detailed comparisons between fuzzy sets that take similarity levels into account.Improved decision-making**: Provide thorough comparisons in the face of uncertainty to increase decision accuracy. Entropy measure: Quantifying uncertainty: To improve decision-making, quantify the level of fuzziness or uncertainty inside a set.Optimising systems: Support feature selection and fuzzy system optimization to improve model performance. Inclusion measure: Quantifying subset relationships: Determine the exact extent to which a fuzzy set contains another fuzzy set.Enhanced analysis and decision-making: Promote customized decision-making and improve comprehension of ambiguous relationships.

Precedence of case study results

Predictive research on preeclampsia improves maternal and fetal health outcomes while lowering healthcare expenditures and complications through early detection and intervention. It makes individualized treatment easier and improves overall pregnancy care.The development of micro-surgical robot manufacturing techniques improves patient outcomes and surgical precision while lowering costs and increasing accessibility to cutting-edge medical equipment. It also promotes minimally invasive procedures, which shorten recovery times and lower complications.

Limitations and challenges

Complexity of implementation—The novel measures may be difficult to apply and need sophisticated computing power, which might prevent them from being used in some real-world situations.Limited previous research—Researchers and practitioners who are new to this topic may face a high learning curve because of the paucity of previous research on LDMFS.Validation and generalisation—Although case studies have been used to validate the suggested metrics, more investigation is required to guarantee that they can be applied to various datasets and domains.Data dependency—The quality and accessibility of data are essential to the efficacy of the suggested techniques, especially in the diagnoses of medical conditions where data variability can have a substantial influence on outcomes.

Complexity of implementation—The novel measures may be difficult to apply and need sophisticated computing power, which might prevent them from being used in some real-world situations.

Managerial implications

There are important managerial ramifications when using Linear Diophantine Multi-fuzzy Sets (LDMFS) in decision-making processes.Enhanced decision-making: By providing a multifaceted method for handling complicated and ambiguous data, LDMFS increases the accuracy and dependability of managerial judgments. By streamlining the computation process and combining multiple parameters into a single model, this method enables more thorough and precise analysis.Cost efficiency: LDMFS lowers operational expenses by eliminating the need for protracted trial-and-error procedures by offering an organized and effective technique for handling imprecise data. By using fewer resources and better information, managers are able to maximize their time and financial commitments.Strategic planning: Better strategic planning is made possible by the capacity to handle and evaluate multifaceted data. LDMFS can be used in a variety of situations to help managers foresee future trends and get ready for any obstacles, which improves the organization’s overall strategic foresight.Adaptability and innovation: LDMFS’s scalability and flexibility promote innovation inside the company. To keep the company competitive and sensitive to shifts in the market or industry, managers might modify the framework to fit new uses and new problems.Operational efficiency: LDMFS makes problem-solving procedures simpler and makes it easier to integrate complicated systems. Managers are thus able to use solutions that are straightforward to adopt across various departments and functions, which improves operational efficiency.Organisations can attain improved decision-making accuracy, cost savings, strategic agility, inventive capabilities, and overall operational excellence by utilising the benefits of LDMFS.

Enhanced decision-making: By providing a multifaceted method for handling complicated and ambiguous data, LDMFS increases the accuracy and dependability of managerial judgments. By streamlining the computation process and combining multiple parameters into a single model, this method enables more thorough and precise analysis.

Adaptability and innovation: LDMFS’s scalability and flexibility promote innovation inside the company. To keep the company competitive and sensitive to shifts in the market or industry, managers might modify the framework to fit new uses and new problems.

Operational efficiency: LDMFS makes problem-solving procedures simpler and makes it easier to integrate complicated systems. Managers are thus able to use solutions that are straightforward to adopt across various departments and functions, which improves operational efficiency.

Conclusion

Fuzzy set theory has advanced significantly with the creation of novel similarity measures for linear diophantine multi-fuzzy sets, including cosine, Jaccard, and exponential similarity measures, in addition to entropy, inclusion, and distance measures. In along with introducing these unique metrics, this study outlines the theoretical links among them, offering a strong basis for further investigation and implementation. The usefulness and adaptability of these measures in handling challenging decision-making scenarios are highlighted by the real-world applications of this research, which include diagnosing preeclampsia in pregnant women, improving pattern recognition, and optimizing material selection for medical surgical robots. Introducing linear diophantine multi-fuzzy sets to existing fuzzy set theories makes significant tools for sophisticated analysis and decision-making possible, especially in vital fields like advanced manufacturing and healthcare. The comparative analysis and superiority analysis shows the effectiveness of our model.

In the future, efforts will concentrate on using sophisticated decision-making techniques like TOPSIS, PROMETHEE, and VIKOR within the context of linear diophantine multi-fuzzy sets. By using these techniques, the decision-making process will be further improved and refined, leading to more solid and thorough solutions. In addition, research into many applications in the technical and medical domains will persist with the goal of fully utilising these sophisticated fuzzy set measures. Future work in this area will try to establish the usefulness of linear diophantine multi-fuzzy sets in a variety of intricate and high-stakes situations, encouraging creativity and enhancing results in a variety of fields.
